width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}\crcr}}\kern 1366.13223pt}}$}\;}\right)

but

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}2,&0\\
1,&1\end{matrix}\biggr)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\geq 1,\,c,d\geq 2\\
a+b+c+d=w\end{subarray}}\zeta\left(\;{\footnotesize\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$d$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}\cr\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$a$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$b$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$c$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}\crcr}}\kern 1366.13223pt}}$}\;}\right)\neq S_{w}\left(\;{\footnotesize\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\kern 0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\kern 0.33994pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}\cr\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}&\lower 0.33994pt\vbox{\kern 0.16997pt\hbox{\kern 0.33994pt\vbox to10.54005pt{\vss\hbox to10.20012pt{\hss$\,$\hss}\vss}\kern-10.54005pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\vrule height=10.54005pt,width=0.33994pt}\kern-0.16997pt\kern-10.54005pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt\kern 10.20012pt\hrule width=10.87999pt,height=0.33994pt}\crcr}}\kern 1366.13223pt}}$}\;}\right).

In the latter, the index
(d)
is required to be admissible, i.e.,
d\geq 2
.

Note that
S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&\ldots,&s_{n}\\
r_{1},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)
is nonzero
only when

(3.14)

w\geq s_{1}+\cdots+s_{n}+r_{1}+\cdots+r_{n}+n.

Our basic strategy of computing these sums on ribbons is
to reduce the number of corners
n
by using the following formula:

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\geq 0
and
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}>0
be integers.
For
1\leq i\leq n-1
with
r_{i}\geq 2
, we have

(3.15)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&\ldots,&s_{i},&s_{i+1},&\ldots,&s_{n}\\
r_{1},&\ldots,&r_{i},&r_{i+1},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)+S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&\ldots,&s_{i},&s_{i+1}+1,&\ldots,&s_{n}\\
r_{1},&\ldots,&r_{i}-1,&r_{i+1},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)

(3.16)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}+w_{2}=w\\
w_{1}\geq s_{1}+\cdots+s_{i}+r_{1}+\cdots+r_{i}+i\\
w_{2}\geq s_{i+1}+\cdots+s_{n}+r_{i+1}+\cdots+r_{n}+n-i\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&\ldots,&s_{i}\\
r_{1},&\ldots,&r_{i}\end{matrix}\biggr)\cdot S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{i+1},&\ldots,&s_{n}\\
r_{i+1},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr).

Proof.

By switching the inequality
b_{i+1,1}<a_{i,1}
in (
3.3
) (for the given
i
)
to the opposite
a_{i,1}\leq b_{i+1,1}
and adding the corresponding Schur MZVs, we deduce that

(3.18)

\zeta\biggl(\begin{matrix}{\bm{l}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{i},&{\bm{l}}_{i+1},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{n}\\
{\bm{k}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{i},&{\bm{k}}_{i+1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)+\zeta\biggl(\begin{matrix}{\bm{l}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{i},&{\bm{l}}_{i+1}^{\prime},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{n}\\
{\bm{k}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{i}^{\prime},&{\bm{k}}_{i+1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)\\
=\zeta\biggl(\begin{matrix}{\bm{l}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{i}\\
{\bm{k}}_{1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{i}\end{matrix}\biggr)\cdot\zeta\biggl(\begin{matrix}{\bm{l}}_{i+1},&\ldots,&{\bm{l}}_{n}\\
{\bm{k}}_{i+1},&\ldots,&{\bm{k}}_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr),

where
{\bm{k}}_{i}^{\prime}=(k_{i,2},\ldots,k_{i,r_{i}})
and
{\bm{l}}_{i+1}^{\prime}=(k_{i,1},l_{i+1,1},\ldots,l_{i+1,s_{i+1}})
.
Then (
3.15
) follows.
∎

By using Proposition
3.3
repeatedly,
the sums on general ribbons are expressed in terms of the values
of the type
S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{1},&r_{2},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)
.
For example, we have

(3.19)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&1\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}+w_{2}=w\\
w_{1}\geq s+r_{1}+2\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+1\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s\\
r_{1}+1\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}0\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)-S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0\\
r_{1}+1,&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr),

(3.20)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&2\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}+w_{2}=w\\
w_{1}\geq s+r_{1}+2\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+2\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s\\
r_{1}+1\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}1\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)-S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&1\\
r_{1}+1,&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

(3.21)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}+w_{2}=w\\
w_{1}\geq s+r_{1}+2\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+2\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s\\
r_{1}+1\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}1\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

(3.22)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}+w_{2}=w\\
w_{1}\geq s+r_{1}+e\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+1\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s\\
r_{1}+2\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}0\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)+S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0\\
r_{1}+2,&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

and so on (cf. Lemma
3.11
).
For the latter type sums, the following formula holds:

Theorem 3.4
.

For
w\geq 0
,
s\geq 0
and
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}>0
, we have

(3.23)

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{1},&r_{2},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+\cdots+t_{n}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r_{i}+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{n}=w\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{w_{i}-1}{t_{i}}\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}).

Proof.

Put
r\coloneqq r_{1}+\cdots+r_{n}
.
Then the left hand side is the sum of the series

\zeta\left(\;\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$k_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule width=0.0pt,height=12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\kern 0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\kern 0.39993pt}\nullfont&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$\raisebox{-2.0pt}{$\vdots$}$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$l_{1}$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$\cdots$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$l_{s}$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to12.39992pt{\vss\hbox to11.99998pt{\hss$k_{r}$\hss}\vss}\kern-12.39992pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\vrule height=12.39992pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-12.39992pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 11.99998pt\hrule width=12.79985pt,height=0.39993pt}\crcr}}\kern 1821.37631pt}}$}\;\right)=\sum_{0<a_{1}<\cdots<a_{r}\geq b_{s}\geq\cdots\geq b_{1}>0}\frac{1}{a_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots a_{r}^{k_{r}}b_{1}^{l_{1}}\cdots b_{s}^{l_{s}}},

where
(l_{1},\ldots,l_{s})
runs through indices of depth
s
and
(k_{1},\ldots,k_{r})
runs through
indices of depth
r
such that
k_{u}\geq 2
for
u\in\{r_{n},r_{n}+r_{n-1},\ldots,r\}
,
satisfying
l_{1}+\cdots+l_{s}+k_{1}+\cdots+k_{r}=w
.
By “stuffling”, i.e., classifying all possible orders of
a_{p}
’s and
b_{q}
’s,
this series is expanded into a certain sum of MZVs
\zeta(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r+u})

of weight
w
with
0\leq u\leq s
. Here each of entries of
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r+u})
is
of the form

(3.28)

k_{p}+l_{q}+\cdots+l_{q^{\prime}}\text{ or }l_{q}+\cdots+l_{q^{\prime}},

where
l_{q},\ldots,l_{q^{\prime}}
are some consecutive members of
l_{1},\ldots,l_{s}
,
possibly zero members in the former case and at least one member in the latter.

Let us fix an index
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r+u})
of weight
w
with
0\leq u\leq s

and count how many times
\zeta(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r+u})
appears in

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{1},&r_{2},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)

when all series (
3.1
) are expanded as above.
First, given
u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\geq 0
satisfying
u_{1}+\cdots+u_{n}=u
,
consider the cases that

x_{r_{n}+u_{n}+\cdots+r_{i}+u_{i}}
contains
k_{r_{n}+\cdots+r_{i}}
as a summand for
i=1,\ldots,n
.
Then the number of possibilities of the places where other
k_{p}
’s appear is

\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{r_{i}-1+u_{i}}{u_{i}}
. Moreover,
each entry of
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{r+u})
is decomposed into a sum of type (
3.28
)
and the total number of the plus symbol ‘
+
’ is
s-u
.
The number of possible such decompositions is

(3.29)

\displaystyle\binom{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl\{(x_{h_{i+1}+1}-1)+\cdots+(x_{h_{i}-1}-1)+(x_{h_{i}}-2)\bigr\}}{s-u}

(3.30)

\displaystyle=\binom{w-n-r-u}{s-u}

,

where we set
h_{i}=r_{n}+u_{n}+\cdots+r_{i}+u_{i}
and
h_{n+1}=0
.
Therefore we obtain that

(3.31)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{1},&r_{2},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\geq 0\\
u\coloneqq u_{1}+\cdots+u_{n}\leq s\end{subarray}}\binom{w-n-r-u}{s-u}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{r_{i}+u_{i}-1}{u_{i}}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},\ldots,w_{r+u}\geq 1\\
w_{p}\geq 2\;(p=r_{n}+u_{n}+\cdots+r_{i}+u_{i})\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{r+u}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{r+u})

.

By applying Ohno’s relation to the last sum, we see that this is equal to

(3.33)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{1},&r_{2},&\ldots,&r_{n}\end{matrix}\biggr)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\geq 0\\
u\coloneqq u_{1}+\cdots+u_{n}\leq s\end{subarray}}\binom{w-n-r-u}{s-u}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{r_{i}+u_{i}-1}{u_{i}}

(3.34)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r_{i}+u_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{n}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})

.

By means of the identity

(3.35)

\binom{w-n-r-u}{s-u}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\geq 0\\
v_{1}+\cdots+v_{n}=s-u\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{w_{i}-1-r_{i}-u_{i}}{v_{i}},

one can rewrite the expression (
3.33
) as

(3.36)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u_{1},\ldots,u_{n}\geq 0\\
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n}\geq 0\\
u_{1}+v_{1}+\cdots+u_{n}+v_{n}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r_{i}+u_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{n}=w\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{w_{i}-1-r_{i}-u_{i}}{v_{i}}\binom{r_{i}+u_{i}-1}{u_{i}}\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})

(3.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+\cdots+t_{n}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r_{i}+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{n}=w\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\Biggl(\sum_{u_{i}=0}^{t_{i}}\binom{w_{i}-1-r_{i}-u_{i}}{t_{i}-u_{i}}\binom{r_{i}+u_{i}-1}{u_{i}}\Biggr)\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}).

Here we put
t_{i}=u_{i}+v_{i}
and use that

(3.38)

\binom{w_{i}-1-r_{i}-u_{i}}{t_{i}-u_{i}}=0\quad\text{ when $t_{i}>u_{i}$ and }r_{i}+u_{i}+1\leq w_{i}\leq r_{i}+t_{i}.

Thus the theorem follows from the identity

(3.39)

\displaystyle\sum_{u_{i}=0}^{t_{i}}\binom{w_{i}-1-r_{i}-u_{i}}{t_{i}-u_{i}}\binom{r_{i}+u_{i}-1}{u_{i}}

\displaystyle=\binom{w_{i}-1}{t_{i}}.\qed

Corollary 3.5
.

For
n\geq 1
and integers
s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}\geq 0
and
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}>0
the sum

S_{w}\binom{s_{1},\ldots,s_{n}}{r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}}

can be written as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of MZVs of weight
w
and depth
\leq n
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Proposition
3.3
and Theorem
3.4
.
∎

Corollary 3.6
.

For any
s\geq 0
and
r_{1},\ldots,r_{n}>0
, the symmetric sum

(3.40)

\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r_{\sigma(1)},&r_{\sigma(2)},&\ldots,&r_{\sigma(n)}\end{matrix}\biggr)

is a polynomial in single zeta values. In particular,

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0,&\ldots,&0\\
r,&r,&\ldots,&r\end{matrix}\biggr)

is a polynomial in single zeta values.

Proof.

By (
3.23
), this symmetric sum is equal to

(3.41)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},\ldots,t_{n}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+\cdots+t_{n}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r_{i}+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{n}=w\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{w_{i}-1}{t_{i}}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{n}}\zeta(w_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,w_{\sigma(n)}).

Thus the claim follows from Hoffman’s symmetric sum formula
[
Hoffman92
, Theorem 2.2]
.
∎

Example 3.7
.

The case of
n=1
will be treated in Theorem
3.8
.
Here let us consider the case of
n=2
and
r_{1}=r_{2}=r
.
We have

(3.42)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s,&0\\
r,&r\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

(3.43)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\bigl(\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})+\zeta(w_{2},w_{1})\bigr)

(3.44)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{i}\geq r+t_{i}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\bigl(\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})-\zeta(w)\bigr).

This is a kind of sum formula of polynomial type.
For a sum formula of bounded type, see §
3.3
(for instance see Example
3.16
for a hook case).

3.2.
Sum formulas of single type

In this subsection,
we present sum formulas of single type for two special types of ribbons.
The first is a simultaneous generalization of the classical sum formulas
for the MZVs and MZSVs stated in (
1.2
).

Theorem 3.8
.

For any integers
r\geq 1
,
s\geq 0
and
w\geq s+r+1
, we have

(3.45)

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s\\
r\end{matrix}\biggr)=\binom{w-1}{s}\zeta(w).

Proof.

This immediately follows from Theorem
3.4
.
∎

The second formula is for the “stair of tread one” shape.
The proof is an application of Proposition
3.3

and Theorem
3.4
.

Theorem 3.9
.

For any integers
r\geq 1
,
n\geq 1
and
w\geq(r+2)n+1
, we have

(3.46)

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}\{1\}^{n-1},&1\\
\{r\}^{n-1},&r+1\end{matrix}\biggr)=c_{w,r}(n)\zeta(w),

where

(3.47)

c_{w,r}(n)\coloneqq\frac{w-1}{n}\binom{w-(r+1)n-2}{n-1}.

See (
1
) in the introduction for examples in the cases
r=n=2
and
r=1
,
n=3
.

Remark 3.10
.

The coefficient
c_{w,r}(n)
is a positive integer. In fact,

(3.48)

c_{w,r}(n)=(r+1)\binom{w-(r+1)n-2}{n-1}+\binom{w-(r+1)n-1}{n}.

Proof of Theorem
3.9
.

We prove (
3.46
) by induction on
n
.
For
n=1
, this is a special case of (
3.45
).
Let us assume
n>1
and for
1\leq i\leq n
, put

(3.49)

S_{w,r}(n,i)\coloneqq S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}\{1\}^{i-1},&1,&\{0\}^{n-i}\\
\{r\}^{i-1},&r+1,&\{r+1\}^{n-i}\end{matrix}\biggr),\qquad T_{w,r}(n)\coloneqq S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}\{0\}^{n}\\
\{r+1\}^{n}\end{matrix}\biggr).

Then, for
1\leq i\leq n-1
, Proposition
3.3
shows that

(3.50)

S_{w,r}(n,i)+S_{w,r}(n,i+1)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq(r+2)i+1\\
w_{2}\geq(r+2)(n-i)\end{subarray}}S_{w_{1},r}(i,i)\cdot T_{w_{2},r}(n-i)

(here and in what follows, we omit the “total weight
=w
” condition like
w_{1}+w_{2}=w
).
The induction hypothesis gives
S_{w_{1},r}(i,i)=c_{w_{1},r}(i)\zeta(w_{1})
for
1\leq i\leq n-1
,
while Theorem
3.4
shows that

(3.51)

T_{w_{2},r}(n-i)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i}\geq r+2\\
w^{\prime}_{1}+\cdots+w^{\prime}_{n-i}=w_{2}\end{subarray}}\zeta(w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i}).

Hence we obtain

\begin{split}S_{w,r}(n,i)+S_{w,r}(n,i+1)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq(r+2)i+1\\
w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i}\geq r+2\end{subarray}}c_{w_{1},r}(i)\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i})\\
&=A_{i}+B_{i},\end{split}

where
A_{i}
and
B_{i}
are defined by

\displaystyle A_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq(r+2)i+1\\
w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i}\geq r+2\end{subarray}}c_{w_{1},r}(i)\bigl\{\zeta(w_{1},w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i})+\cdots+\zeta(w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i},w_{1})\bigr\}

(3.52)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n-i+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},\ldots,w_{n-i+1}\geq r+2\\
w_{j}\geq(r+2)i+1\end{subarray}}c_{w_{j},r}(i)\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n-i+1})

and

(3.53)

\displaystyle B_{i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq(r+2)i+1\\
w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i}\geq r+2\end{subarray}}c_{w_{1},r}(i)\bigl\{\zeta(w_{1}+w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{n-i})+\cdots+\zeta(w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w_{1}+w^{\prime}_{n-i})\bigr\}

(3.54)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n-i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},\ldots,w_{n-i}\geq r+2\\
w_{j}\geq(r+2)(i+1)+1\end{subarray}}\sum_{a=(r+2)i+1}^{w_{j}-(r+2)}c_{a,r}(i)\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n-i}).

Note that the definition of
A_{i}
works for
i=n
and gives
A_{n}=c_{w,r}(n)\zeta(w)
.

For
1\leq i\leq n-1
, we have
A_{i+1}=B_{i}
since

(3.55)

\displaystyle\sum_{a=(r+2)i+1}^{w_{j}-(r+2)}c_{a,r}(i)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b=0}^{k}\frac{(r+2)i+b}{i}\binom{b+i-1}{i-1}\qquad(k\coloneqq w_{j}-(r+2)(i+1)-1)

(3.56)

\displaystyle=(r+1)\sum_{b=0}^{k}\binom{b+i-1}{i-1}+\sum_{b=0}^{k}\binom{b+i}{i}

(3.57)

\displaystyle=(r+1)\binom{k+i}{i}+\binom{k+i+1}{i+1}=c_{w_{j},r}(i+1).

Thus (
3.2
) says that

(3.58)

S_{w,r}(n,i)+S_{w,r}(n,i+1)=A_{i}+A_{i+1}.

This implies inductively that
S_{w,r}(n,i)=A_{i}
, starting from

(3.59)

S_{w,r}(n,1)=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},\ldots,w_{n}\geq r+2\\
w_{j}\geq r+3\end{subarray}}(w_{j}-1)\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{n})=A_{1},

which is a consequence of (
3.23
) and (
3.52
).
The final identity
S_{w,r}(n,n)=A_{n}=c_{w,r}(n)\zeta(w)
is exactly the desired formula.
∎

3.3.
Two corners

We next study ribbon shapes with two corners which give a bounded type sum formula.
The key ingredient is the sum formula weighted by the binomial coefficients
(
2.1
).
For ribbons with two corners, we only use the case
d=2
.
For ease of calculation,
we rewrite
2.6
in the following form
with
m_{1}=k_{1}-1
and
m_{2}=k_{2}-1
:
For integers
m_{1},m_{2}\geq 0
and
w\geq m_{1}+m_{2}+3
,
we have

(3.60)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{m_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{m_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m_{2}+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{m_{2}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{m_{1}+m_{2}+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

\displaystyle+(-1)^{m_{1}+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{m_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{m_{1}+m_{2}+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

\displaystyle+\mathbbm{1}_{m_{2}=0}\binom{w-2}{m_{1}}\bigl(\zeta(w)+\zeta(1,w-1)\bigr).

We then start with a preliminary calculation:

Lemma 3.11
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0
,
r_{1},r_{2}>0
and
w\geq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}+2
, we have

(3.61)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-i+1\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+i+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.62)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{1}+1\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+t_{2}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}).

Proof.

By a repeated application of Proposition
3.3
,
we obtain

(3.63)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{w_{1}+w_{2}=w}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1}\\
r_{1}+1\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{2}-1\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)-S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}-1\\
r_{1}+1,&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

(3.64)

\displaystyle=\cdots

(3.65)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{s_{2}}(-1)^{i-1}\sum_{w_{1}+w_{2}=w}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1}\\
r_{1}+i\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{2}-i\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)+(-1)^{s_{2}}S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&0\\
s_{2}+r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

(3.66)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{w_{1}+w_{2}=w}S_{w_{1}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1}\\
s_{2}+r_{1}-i\end{matrix}\biggr)S_{w_{2}}\biggl(\begin{matrix}i\\
r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)+(-1)^{s_{2}}S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&0\\
s_{2}+r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr).

By applying Theorem
3.8

to the first sum with taking care of admissible range
and by applying Theorem
3.4

to the second sum, we obtain the lemma.
∎

As an immediate consequence of the expression above,
one finds that there is indeed a polynomial type sum formula
for a specific class of ribbons with two corners, that is, the case
r_{2}=s_{2}+r_{1}
.

Theorem 3.12
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0
,
r_{1}>0
and
w\geq s_{1}+2s_{2}+2r_{1}+2
, we have

(3.67)

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}\\
r_{1},&s_{2}+r_{1}\end{matrix}\biggr)

is a polynomial in single zeta values.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.11
with
r_{2}=s_{2}+r_{1}
,
it suffices to show

(3.68)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{1}+1\\
w_{2}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{2}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

can be written as a polynomial of single zeta values.
By symmetry, this sum is

(3.69)

=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{1}+1\\
w_{2}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{2}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\bigl(\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})+\zeta(w_{2},w_{1})\bigr).

Then, the result follows by the harmonic product formula.
∎

The next theorem is a sum formula for general ribbons with two corners, which is of bounded type.
Although the explicit formula itself is rather complicated,
it is a direct consequence of
eq.

3.60
and
lemma

3.11
.

Theorem 3.13
.

For
s_{1},s_{2}\geq 0
,
r_{1},r_{2}>0
and
w\geq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}+r_{2}+2
, we have

(3.70)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\binom{w-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}}\zeta(w)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}A_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}B_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}),

where the integers

A_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}

and

B_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}

are given by

(3.71)

\displaystyle A_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{w_{1}}C_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2}}

(3.72)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\mathbbm{1}_{w_{1}\leq s_{1}+r_{1}\ \textup{or}\ w_{2}\leq s_{2}+r_{2}-1}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{2}-1}

(3.73)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbbm{1}_{w_{1}>s_{1}+r_{1}}(-1)^{s_{1}+r_{1}+w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-w_{1}}

(3.74)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbbm{1}_{r_{2}<w_{2}\leq s_{2}+r_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}+r_{2}+w_{2}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{r_{2}-1},

(3.75)

\displaystyle B_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq C_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2}}-(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}\geq w_{1}-(s_{2}+r_{1})\ \textup{or}\ t_{2}\geq w_{2}-r_{2}\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}

with

(3.76)

C_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2}}\coloneqq(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq s_{1}\\
1\leq j\leq s_{2}\\
i+j=w_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}-i}-(-1)^{s_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}-w_{1}}.

Remark 3.14
.

By our convention on binomial coefficients,

A_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}=B_{w_{1},w_{2}}^{s_{1},s_{2},r_{1},r_{2}}=0
unless

(3.77)

w_{1}\leq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}\quad\text{ or }\quad w_{2}\leq\max(s_{2}+r_{2}-1,s_{1}+r_{2})

and so
eq.

3.70

is a bounded type sum formula
after expanding the product
\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})
by the harmonic product formula.

Proof of
3.13
.

Write the equation in Lemma
3.11
as

(3.78)

S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}s_{1},&s_{2}\\
r_{1},&r_{2}\end{matrix}\biggr)=S_{1}+S_{2}

with

(3.79)

\displaystyle S_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-i+1\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+i+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2}),

(3.80)

\displaystyle S_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{1}+1\\
w_{2}\geq r_{2}+t_{2}+1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}).

The sum
S_{1}
can be rewritten as
S_{1}=S_{11}-S_{12}-S_{13}
where

(3.81)

\displaystyle S_{11}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.82)

\displaystyle S_{12}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{1}\leq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-i\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.83)

\displaystyle S_{13}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{2}\leq r_{2}+i\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2}).

By the harmonic product formula, we have

S_{11}=S_{111}+S_{112}+S_{113}

with

(3.84)

\displaystyle S_{111}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}),

(3.85)

\displaystyle S_{112}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}),

(3.86)

\displaystyle S_{113}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\biggl\{\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\biggr\}\zeta(w).

For the sum
S_{111}
,
after supplementing the term with
w_{1}=1
,
by
eq.

3.60
, we get

(3.87)

\displaystyle S_{111}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.88)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

(3.89)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}(-1)^{s_{2}-1}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\bigl(\zeta(w)+\zeta(1,w-1)\bigr)

(3.90)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w-2}{i}\zeta(1,w-1),

where we should note that the sum
S_{111}
is empty if
s_{2}=0
and
\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}

is inserted to cover such a degenerate case.
By swapping the summation
and changing variable via
i\leadsto w_{1}-i-1
, we have

(3.91)

\displaystyle(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.92)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq s_{1}\\
1\leq j\leq s_{2}\\
i+j=w_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}-i}.

Since
\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}=0
if
i<w_{1}-s_{1}-1
,
by changing variable via

i\leadsto i+w_{1}-s_{1}-1
, we have

(3.93)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

(3.94)

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}).

Combining the above two formulas with

\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w-2}{i}=\binom{w-3}{s_{2}-1}
,
we have

(3.95)

\displaystyle S_{111}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq s_{1}\\
1\leq i\leq s_{2}\\
i+j=w_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{j}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}-j}

(3.96)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt-(-1)^{s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

(3.97)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}(-1)^{s_{2}}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\bigl(\zeta(w)+\zeta(1,w-1)\bigr)-\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\binom{w-3}{s_{2}-1}\zeta(1,w-1).

Similarly, for the sum
S_{112}
,
we can apply
eq.

3.60

to get

(3.98)

\displaystyle S_{112}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{1}+s_{2}-i}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.99)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}+i+1-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})

(3.100)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w-2}{i}\bigl(\zeta(w)+\zeta(1,w-1)\bigr)

(3.101)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}(-1)^{s_{2}}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\zeta(1,w-1).

By a calculation similar to that of
S_{111}
, we have

(3.102)

\displaystyle S_{112}

\displaystyle=-(-1)^{s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.103)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq i\leq s_{1}\\
1\leq j\leq s_{2}\\
i+j=w_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{i}\binom{w_{2}-1}{s_{1}-i}

(3.104)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\binom{w-3}{s_{2}-1}\bigl(\zeta(w)+\zeta(1,w-1)\bigr)+\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}(-1)^{s_{2}}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\zeta(1,w-1).

For the sum
S_{113}
,
by
\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}=\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{2}-1}
we have

(3.105)

\displaystyle S_{113}

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}\biggr\}\zeta(w)

(3.106)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{2}-1}-\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\binom{w-3}{s_{2}-1}\biggr\}\zeta(w)

(3.107)

\displaystyle=\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{s_{2}>0}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}}-\mathbbm{1}_{s_{1}=0}\binom{w-3}{s_{2}-1}\biggr\}\zeta(w).

We next consider the sum
S_{12}
and
S_{13}
.
By swapping the summation, we have

(3.108)

\displaystyle S_{12}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{1}\leq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})\sum_{i=0}^{\min(s_{2}-1,s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-w_{1})}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}

(3.109)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{1}\leq s_{1}+r_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})\sum_{i=0}^{s_{2}-1}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}

(3.110)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1}+r_{1}<w_{1}\leq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})\sum_{i=0}^{s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-w_{1}}(-1)^{s_{2}-i-1}\binom{w_{2}-1}{i}

(3.111)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{1}\leq s_{1}+r_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{2}-1}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.112)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}s_{1}+r_{1}<w_{1}\leq s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{s_{1}+r_{1}+w_{1}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{1}+s_{2}+r_{1}-w_{1}}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2}).

Similarly, we have

(3.113)

\displaystyle S_{13}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{2}\leq s_{2}+r_{2}-1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{s_{2}-1}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2})

(3.114)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{2}<w_{2}\leq s_{2}+r_{2}-1\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}(-1)^{s_{2}+r_{2}+w_{2}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{s_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-2}{r_{2}-1}\zeta(w_{1})\zeta(w_{2}).

For the sum
S_{2}
, we can dissect as

S_{2}=S_{21}-S_{22}-S_{23}

by writing

(3.115)

\displaystyle S_{21}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}),

(3.116)

\displaystyle S_{22}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq w_{1}\leq s_{2}+r_{1}+t_{1}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}),

(3.117)

\displaystyle S_{23}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}2\leq w_{2}\leq r_{2}+t_{2}\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2}).

For
S_{21}
, we can calculate the sum over binomial coefficients and get

(3.118)

\displaystyle S_{21}=(-1)^{s_{2}}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})=(-1)^{s_{2}}\binom{w-2}{s_{1}}\zeta(w)

by the usual sum formula.
For
S_{22},S_{23}
, note that

(3.119)

\displaystyle S_{22}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}\geq w_{1}-(s_{2}+r_{1})\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}},

(3.120)

\displaystyle S_{23}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{2}\geq w_{2}-r_{2}\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}.

Since the range of
(t_{1},t_{2})
for
S_{22}
and
S_{23}
are disjoint, we have

(3.121)

\displaystyle S_{22}+S_{23}=(-1)^{s_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1}\geq 1,w_{2}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+w_{2}=w\end{subarray}}\zeta(w_{1},w_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0\\
t_{1}\geq w_{1}-(s_{2}+r_{1})\ \text{or}\ t_{2}\geq w_{2}-r_{2}\\
t_{1}+t_{2}=s_{1}\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{t_{1}}\binom{w_{2}-1}{t_{2}}.

By combining the above calculations, we obtain the theorem.
∎

As a special case of Theorem
3.13
,
we obtain the following sum formula of bounded type
for the hook shape. Note that the left hand side of the next corollary corresponds to the hook shape
(s,1^{r})
when
s\geq 2
.

Corollary 3.15
.

For
s,r\geq 1
and
w\geq s+r+2
, we have

(3.122)

\displaystyle S_{w}\biggl(\begin{matrix}0,&s-1\\
r,&1\end{matrix}\biggr)

\displaystyle=\binom{w-2}{s-1}\zeta(w)-\sum^{s-1}_{k=1}\binom{w-k-2}{s-k-1}\zeta(k,w-k)+(-1)^{s}\sum^{s+r-1}_{k=s}\zeta(k,w-k)

(3.123)

\displaystyle\ \ \ -\sum^{s-1}_{k=2}(-1)^{k}\binom{w-k-2}{s-k-1}\zeta(k)\zeta(w-k)-\sum^{r}_{k=2}\binom{w-k-2}{s-2}\zeta(k)\zeta(w-k)

(3.124)

\displaystyle\ \ \ +(-1)^{r}\sum^{s+r-1}_{k=r+1}(-1)^{k}\binom{w-k-2}{r+s-1-k}\zeta(k)\zeta(w-k).

Proof.

Follows from
3.13

and some rearrangement of terms.
∎

Example 3.16
.

For the shape
\lambda=(3,1,1)
we obtain for
w\geq 7
the sum formula

(3.128)

(3.129)

from
3.15
bytaking
s=3
and
r=2
.

4.
Diagrams with one corner

In this section, we consider general shapes with one corner. Recall that in Section
2
we defined for an index
{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})
and
l\geq 0

(4.1)

\displaystyle Q_{l}({\bm{k}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{w}}=(w_{1},\ldots,w_{d}):\text{ adm.}\\
\wt({\bm{w}})=\wt({\bm{k}})+l\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{k_{1}-1}\cdots\binom{w_{d-1}-1}{k_{d-1}-1}\binom{w_{d}-2}{k_{d}-1}\zeta({\bm{w}})\,.

We will show in Theorem
4.2
that our target
S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)
defined in (
1
) with
\lambda/\mu
having one corner can be expressed explicitly in terms of
Q_{w-|\lambda/\mu|}
. This will follow from a purely combinatorial argument and we will see that this statement is already true for truncated Schur MZVs. For an integer
M\geq 1
and an arbitrary, not necessarily admissible, Young tableau
{\bm{k}}
, these are defined by

(4.2)

\displaystyle\zeta_{M}({\bm{k}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{i,j})\in\SSYT(\lambda/\penalty\mu)\\
m_{i,j}<M\end{subarray}}\prod_{(i,j)\in D(\lambda/\penalty\mu)}\frac{1}{m_{i,j}^{k_{i,j}}}\,.

In particular, for an integer
M\geq 1
and an index
{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})
with
k_{1},\dots,k_{d}\geq 1
the truncated MZVs are given by

(4.3)

\displaystyle\zeta_{M}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})=\sum_{0<m_{1}<\dots<m_{d}<M}\frac{1}{m_{1}^{k_{1}}\dots m_{d}^{k_{d}}}\,.

To give the precise statement of above mentioned theorem, we will need to introduce some algebraic setup following
[
Hoffman97
]
.
Denote by
\mathfrak{H}^{1}=\mathbb{Q}\langle z_{k}\mid k\geq 1\rangle
the non-commutative polynomial ring in the variables
z_{k}
for
k\geq 1
. A monic monomial in
\mathfrak{H}^{1}
is called a word and the empty word will be denoted by
{\bf 1}
.
For
k\geq 1
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\displaystyle z_{k}^{n}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\underbrace{z_{k}\cdots z_{k}}_{n}&\text{if $n>0$},\\[19.91692pt]
\mathbf{1}&\text{if $n=0$},\\[5.69054pt]
0&\text{if $n<0$}.\end{array}\right.

There is a one-to-one correspondence between indices and words;
to each index
{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d})
corresponds the word

z_{\bm{k}}\coloneqq z_{k_{1}}\cdots z_{k_{d}}
.
Thus we can extend various functions on indices to
\mathbb{Q}
-linear maps on
\mathfrak{H}^{1}
.
For example, we define
Q_{l}\colon\mathfrak{H}^{1}\to\mathbb{R}
by setting
Q_{l}(z_{\bm{k}})=Q_{l}({\bm{k}})
and
extending it linearly.

We define the stuffle product
\ast
and the index shuffle product
\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}
on
\mathfrak{H}^{1}
as the
\mathbb{Q}
-bilinear products, which satisfy
{\bf 1}\ast w=w\ast{\bf 1}=w
and
{\bf 1}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}w=w\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}{\bf 1}=w
for any word
w\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}
and for any
i,j\geq 1
and words
w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}
,

(4.7)

\displaystyle z_{i}w_{1}\ast z_{j}w_{2}

\displaystyle=z_{i}(w_{1}\ast z_{j}w_{2})+z_{j}(z_{i}w_{1}\ast w_{2})+z_{i+j}(w_{1}\ast w_{2})\,,

(4.8)

\displaystyle z_{i}w_{1}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{j}w_{2}

\displaystyle=z_{i}(w_{1}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{j}w_{2})+z_{j}(z_{i}w_{1}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}w_{2})\,.

By
[
Hoffman97
, Theorem 2.1]
we obtain a commutative
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra
\mathfrak{H}^{1}_{\ast}
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
D_{1},\ldots,D_{r}
be non-empty subsets of the skew Young diagram
D(\lambda/\mu)
which gives a disjoint decomposition
of
D(\lambda/\mu)
, i.e.,
D(\lambda/\mu)=D_{1}\sqcup\dots\sqcup D_{r}
.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

(i)

If we set
t_{ij}=a
for
(i,j)\in D_{a}
with
a=1,\ldots,r
,
then
(t_{ij})
is a semi-standard Young tableau of shape
\lambda/\mu
.

(ii)

There exists a semi-standard Young tableau
(m_{ij})
of shape
\lambda/\mu
such that

(4.9)

m_{ij}<m_{kl}\iff a<b

holds for any
(i,j)\in D_{a}
and
(k,l)\in D_{b}
.

Proof.

Obviously, (i) implies (ii). Conversely, assume that a semi-standard tableau
(m_{ij})
satisfies
the condition in (ii). If
(t_{ij})
is defined as in (i), one has

(4.10)

m_{ij}<m_{kl}\iff a<b\iff t_{ij}<t_{kl}

for any
(i,j)\in D_{a}
and
(k,l)\in D_{b}
, which shows that
(t_{ij})
is also semi-standard.
Thus (ii) implies (i).
∎

We call a tuple
(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})
of non-empty subsets of
D(\lambda/\mu)

satisfying the equivalent conditions (i) and (ii) of Lemma
4.1
a
semi-standard decomposition
.
Let
\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)
denote the set of all semi-standard decompositions of
D(\lambda/\mu)
.
Then we define an element
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
of
\mathfrak{H}^{1}
by

(4.11)

\varphi(\lambda/\mu)\coloneqq\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}z_{\lvert D_{1}\rvert}\cdots z_{\lvert D_{r}\rvert},

where
\lvert D_{i}\rvert
denotes the number of elements of
D_{i}
.
This element is related to the sum formula as follows.

Theorem 4.2
.

When
\lambda/\mu
has only one corner, we have for
w>\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert

(4.12)

S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)=Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert}(\varphi(\lambda/\mu)).

Proof.

For any admissible Young tableau
{\bm{k}}=(k_{ij})
of shape
\lambda/\mu
, we see that

(4.13)

\zeta({\bm{k}})=\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}\zeta\Biggl(\sum_{(i,j)\in D_{1}}k_{ij},\ldots,\sum_{(i,j)\in D_{r}}k_{ij}\Biggr)

by classifying the semi-standard tableaux
(m_{ij})
of shape
\lambda/\mu

according to the semi-standard decompositions
(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})
determined as in Lemma
4.1
(ii).
Then, from the definition of
S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)
, we have

(4.14)

\displaystyle S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}\in\YT(\lambda/\penalty\mu)\\
{\bm{k}}:\text{ adm.}\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=w\end{subarray}}\zeta({\bm{k}})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{ij}\geq 1,\,(i,j)\in D(\lambda/\mu)\\
k_{ij}\geq 2,\,(i,j)\in C(\lambda/\mu)\\
\sum_{(i,j)}k_{ij}=w\end{subarray}}\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}\zeta\Biggl(\sum_{(i,j)\in D_{1}}k_{ij},\ldots,\sum_{(i,j)\in D_{r}}k_{ij}\Biggr)

(4.15)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}w_{1},\ldots,w_{r-1}\geq 1\\
w_{r}\geq 2\\
w_{1}+\cdots+w_{r}=w\end{subarray}}\binom{w_{1}-1}{\lvert D_{1}\rvert-1}\cdots\binom{w_{r-1}-1}{\lvert D_{r-1}\rvert-1}\binom{w_{r}-2}{\lvert D_{r}\rvert-1}\zeta(w_{1},\ldots,w_{r}).

Here recall that
C(\lambda/\mu)
is the set of corners of
\lambda/\mu
and note that, for any
(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)
, the unique corner of
\lambda/\mu
belongs to
D_{r}
.
The last expression above is equal to

(4.16)

\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert}(\lvert D_{1}\rvert,\ldots,\lvert D_{r}\rvert)=Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert}(\varphi(\lambda/\mu)).

Thus the proof is complete.
∎

By Remark
2.2
we see that Theorem
4.2
gives a sum formula of bounded type for shapes with one corner. In order to evaluate the sum
S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)
in the one corner case,
one therefore needs to find an expression of
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
.
For this purpose, the following expression is useful:

Proposition 4.3
.

For any skew shape
\lambda/\mu
, let
\lambda^{\prime}=(\lambda^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\lambda^{\prime}_{s})

and
\mu^{\prime}=(\mu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{\prime}_{s})
be the conjugates of
\lambda
and
\mu
, respectively.
Then we have the identity

(4.17)

\varphi(\lambda/\mu)=\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq s},

where
\det\nolimits_{*}
denotes the determinant performed in the stuffle algebra
\mathfrak{H}^{1}_{*}
.

Proof.

For any integer
M>0
, we have

(4.18)

\zeta_{M}(\varphi(\lambda/\mu))=\sum_{(D_{1},\ldots,D_{r})\in\mathrm{SSD}(\lambda/\mu)}\zeta_{M}(\lvert D_{1}\rvert,\ldots,\lvert D_{r}\rvert)=\zeta_{M}(\mathbf{O}_{\lambda/\mu}),

where
\mathbf{O}_{\lambda/\mu}
denotes the tableau of shape
\lambda/\mu
all entries of which are
1
.
Indeed, the first equality is obvious from the definition of
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
,
and the second follows in the same way as (
4.13
).

On the other hand, by the Jacobi-Trudi type formula for truncated Schur MZVs
(
[
NakasujiPhuksuwanYamasaki2018
, Theorem 1.1]
,
[
Bachmann2018
, Theorem 4.7]
), we have

(4.19)

\zeta_{M}(\mathbf{O}_{\lambda/\mu})=\det\bigl[\zeta_{M}(z_{1}^{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j})\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq s}=\zeta_{M}\Bigl(\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{\lambda^{\prime}_{i}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq s}\Bigr).

Since the map
\mathfrak{H}^{1}\rightarrow\prod_{M\geq 1}\mathbb{Q}
given by
w\mapsto(\zeta_{M}(w))_{M\geq 1}
is
injective (
[
Yamamoto13
, Theorem 3.1]
), we obtain the desired identity in
\mathfrak{H}^{1}
.
∎

In some cases, one can compute
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
explicitly
by using Proposition
4.3
.

Theorem 4.4
.

For
n\geq 1
and
k\geq 0
, it holds that

(4.20)

\displaystyle\varphi\bigl(\,(2^{n+k})/(1^{k})\,\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\frac{k+1}{l+k+1}\binom{2l+k}{l}z_{2}^{n-l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2l+k}.

Proof.

By
4.3
, we have

(4.21)

\displaystyle\varphi\bigl(\,(2^{n+k})/(1^{k})\,\bigr)=\begin{vmatrix}z^{n}_{1}&z^{n+k+1}_{1}\\
z^{n-1}_{1}&z^{n+k}_{1}\end{vmatrix}=z^{n+k}_{1}\ast z^{n}_{1}-z^{n+k+1}_{1}\ast z^{n-1}_{1}.

By
[
Chen15
, Lemma 1]
we get for
m\geq n\geq 1

(4.22)

\displaystyle z^{m}_{1}\ast z^{n}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\binom{m+n-2l}{m-l}\sum_{{\bm{k}}\in G^{m+n-2l}_{l}}z_{\bm{k}}=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\binom{m+n-2l}{n-l}z_{2}^{l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{m+n-2l},

where
G^{a}_{b}
is the set of all possible indices containing
a
times
1
and
b
times
2
. This gives

(4.23)

\displaystyle\varphi\bigl(\,(2^{n+k})/(1^{k})\,\bigr)

(4.24)

\displaystyle=z^{n+k}_{1}\ast z^{n}_{1}-z^{n+k+1}_{1}\ast z^{n-1}_{1}

(4.25)

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\binom{2n+k-2l}{n-l}z_{2}^{l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2n+k-2l}-\sum^{n-1}_{l=0}\binom{2n+k-2l}{n-l-1}z_{2}^{l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2n+k-2l}

(4.26)

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\left(\binom{2n+k-2l}{n-l}-\binom{2n+k-2l}{n-l-1}\right)z_{2}^{l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2n+k-2l}

(4.27)

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\left(\binom{2l+k}{l}-\binom{2l+k}{l-1}\right)z_{2}^{n-l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2l+k}

(4.28)

\displaystyle=\sum^{n}_{l=0}\frac{k+1}{l+k+1}\binom{2l+k}{l}z_{2}^{n-l}\mathbin{\widetilde{\shuffle}}z_{1}^{2l+k}.\qed

For some shapes
\lambda/\mu
, the
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
will contain sums over all indices over a fixed weight and depth. The corresponding sums of
Q
applied to these can be evaluated by using the following lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

For
k\geq d\geq 1,w\geq d+1
and an index
{\bm{n}}=(n_{1},\dots,n_{d})
, we have

(4.29)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=k\end{subarray}}P_{w-k}({\bm{n}};{\bm{k}})=\binom{w-\wt({\bm{n}})}{k-d}\zeta(w).

In particular,

(4.30)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=k\end{subarray}}P_{w-k}({\bm{k}})=\binom{w-d}{k-d}\zeta(w),\qquad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=k\end{subarray}}Q_{w-k}({\bm{k}})=\binom{w-d-1}{k-d}\zeta(w).

Proof.

This follows directly by using the Chu-Vandermonde identity and the usual sum formula for MZVs.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

For
s\geq 0
and
r\geq 1
, one can show the identity

(4.31)

\varphi\big(((s+1)^{r})/(s^{r-1})\big)=\sum^{s}_{l=0}\binom{r+l-1}{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{r+l})\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=r+s\end{subarray}}z_{\bm{k}}

by counting how many times
z_{\bm{k}}
appears for each
{\bm{k}}
, as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
.
(One can also show this by induction on
s
via the expression

\displaystyle\varphi\big(((s+1)^{r})/(s^{r-1})\big)

\displaystyle=\det\nolimits_{*}\!\left[\begin{array}[]{ccccc}z_{1}&z^{2}_{1}&\cdots&z^{s}_{1}&z^{r+s}_{1}\\
1&z_{1}&\cdots&z^{s-1}_{1}&z^{r+s-1}_{1}\\
0&\ddots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\vdots&\ddots&\ddots&z_{1}&z^{r+1}_{1}\\
0&\cdots&0&1&z^{r}_{1}\end{array}\right]

obtained from Proposition
4.3

together with (
4.22
).)
Using Theorem
4.2
and Lemma
4.5
this gives another way of proving the anti-hook sum formula in Theorem
3.8

(4.37)

\displaystyle S_{w}\big(((s+1)^{r})/(s^{r-1})\big)

\displaystyle=Q_{w-(r+s)}\left(\varphi\big(((s+1)^{r})/(s^{r-1})\big)\right)

(4.38)

\displaystyle=\sum^{s}_{l=0}\binom{r+l-1}{l}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\bm{k}}=(k_{1},\ldots,k_{r+l})\\
\wt({\bm{k}})=r+s\end{subarray}}Q_{w-(r+s)}({\bm{k}})

(4.39)

\displaystyle=\sum^{s}_{l=0}\binom{r+l-1}{l}\binom{w-r-l-1}{s-l}\zeta(w)

(4.40)

\displaystyle=\binom{w-1}{s}\zeta(w)\,.

We can summarize the general strategy to give a bounded expression of
S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)
for the case when
\lambda/\mu
has one corner as follows:

(i)

Give an expression for
\varphi(\lambda/\mu)
, by evaluating the determinant in Proposition
4.3
by the stuffle product. Then use Theorem
4.2
to get
S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)=Q_{w-|\lambda/\mu|}(\varphi(\lambda/\mu))
.

(ii)

If sums of
Q_{l}
over all indices of a fixed weight and depth appear, use Lemma
4.5
to write them in terms of Riemann zeta values.

(iii)

For other terms involving
Q
write them in terms of
P
, by using (
2.2
), i.e.,

(4.41)

Q_{l}(k_{1},\dots,k_{d})=\sum^{k_{d}-1}_{j=0}(-1)^{j}P_{l+j}(k_{1},\ldots,k_{d-1},k_{d}-j).

Then use Proposition
2.1
to get recursive (bounded) expressions of
P_{l+j}
in terms of MZVs. For depth
2
and
3
explicit expressions are given by Corollary
2.6
and
2.7
.

.

\begin{equation}\sum_{i\in I}\bigl((i-\mu_{i})-(m-n)\bigr)S_{w}(\lambda/\mu[i])=(w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1)S_{w}(\lambda/\mu).\end{equation}\end{thm}\par Toprovethis,weneedthefollowingLemma.Definealinearmap
∂:

^1
by\begin{equation}\partial(z_{k_{1}}\cdots z_{k_{d}})\coloneqq\sum_{a=1}^{d}k_{a}z_{k_{1}}\cdots z_{k_{a}+1}\cdots z_{k_{d}}.\end{equation}Inparticular,
∂(1)=0
.\par\begin{lemma}\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$\partial$ is a derivation with respect to the stuffle product.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@For any $N\in\mathbb{Z}$, we have
\begin{equation}\partial(z_{1}^{N})=z_{1}*z_{1}^{N}-(N+1)z_{1}^{N+1}.\end{equation}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@We have
\begin{equation}(l-1)Q_{l}(v)=Q_{l-1}(\partial(v))\end{equation}for any $v\in\mathfrak{H}^{1}$.
\end{enumerate}\end{lemma}\@proof\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:partial:derivation}isverified,e.g.,byinductiononthedepth.Itisalsoeasytoshow(ii)fromthedefinition.Finally,theidentityin(iii)followsfromthedefinitionof$Q_{l}$andtheidentity\begin{aligned} &(w-k-1)\binom{w_{1}-1}{k_{1}-1}\cdots\binom{w_{d}-2}{k_{d}-1}\\
&=\bigl((w_{1}-k_{1})+\cdots+(w_{d}-1-k_{d})\bigr)\binom{w_{1}-1}{k_{1}-1}\cdots\binom{w_{d}-2}{k_{d}-1}\\
&=\sum_{a=1}^{d}k_{a}\binom{w_{1}-1}{k_{1}-1}\cdots\binom{w_{a}-1}{k_{a}}\cdots\binom{w_{d}-2}{k_{d}-1},\end{aligned}where$w=w_{1}+\cdots+w_{d}$and$k=k_{1}+\cdots+k_{d}$.\qed\end@proof\par\@proof[Proof of Theorem \ref{thm:S_w rel}]By\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{thm:onecornerwithphi}and\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{prop:jacobitrudiphi},wehave\begin{equation}S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)=Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert}\Bigl(\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\Bigr).\end{equation}Here$(\mu^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\mu^{\prime}_{n})$denotesthetransposeofthepartition$\mu=(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{m})$.Notethat$\mu^{\prime}_{1}=m$and$\mu^{\prime}_{n}>0$.By\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:partial}(iii)and(i),weseethat\begin{aligned} (w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1)S_{w}(\lambda/\mu)&=(w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1)Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert}\Bigl(\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\Bigr)\\
&=Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1}\Bigl(\partial\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\Bigr)\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1}\Bigl(\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[\partial^{\delta_{jk}}(z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j})\bigr]_{1\leq i,j\leq n}\Bigr).\end{aligned}Here$\partial^{\delta_{jk}}$meanstheoperator$\partial$if$j=k$andtheidentityoperatorotherwise.Moreover,sincetheidentity\begin{aligned} \partial(z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{k}-i+k})&=z_{1}*z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{k}-i+k}-(m-\mu^{\prime}_{k}-i+k+1)z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{k}-i+k+1}\\
&=\bigl((\mu^{\prime}_{k}-k)-(m-n)\bigr)z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{k}-1)-i+k}\\
&\qquad+z_{1}*z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{k}-i+k}-(n+1-i)z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{k}-1)-i+k}\end{aligned}holdsby\lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{lem:partial}(ii),wehave\begin{aligned} &\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[\partial^{\delta_{jk}}(z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j})\bigr]\\
&=\bigl((\mu^{\prime}_{k}-k)-(m-n)\bigr)\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr]\\
&\qquad+z_{1}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]-\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[(n+1-i)^{\delta_{jk}}z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr].\end{aligned}\par Ontheotherhand,theleft-handsideof\eqref{eq:S_w rel}isequalto\begin{equation}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\bigl((\mu^{\prime}_{k}-k)-(m-n)\bigr)Q_{w-\lvert\lambda/\mu\rvert-1}\Bigl(\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr]\Bigr).\end{equation}Here,apriori,$k$runsonlyovertheindicessuchthat$\mu^{\prime}_{k}>\mu^{\prime}_{k+1}$.However,if$\mu^{\prime}_{k}=\mu^{\prime}_{k+1}$,the$k$-thand$(k+1)$-stcolumnsofthematrix$\bigl(z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr)_{i,j}$areequal,sothedeterminantiszero.\par Comparing\eqref{eq:S_w rel RHS1},\eqref{eq:S_w rel RHS2}and\eqref{eq:S_w rel LHS},itsufficestoprovetheequality\begin{equation}nz_{1}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]\overset{?}{=}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[(n+1-i)^{\delta_{jk}}z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr].\end{equation}Letuscomputetheright-handsidebythecofactorexpansionwithrespecttothe$k$-thcolumn.\begin{aligned} &\sum_{k=1}^{n}\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[(n+1-i)^{\delta_{jk}}z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{j}-\delta_{jk})-i+j}\bigr]\\
&=\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{n}(-1)^{k+l}(n+1-l)z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{k}-1)-l+k}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{i\neq l,j\neq k}\\
&=\sum_{l=1}^{n}(n+1-l)\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k+l}z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{k}-1)-l+k}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{i\neq l,j\neq k}.\end{aligned}Then,bythecofactorexpansionwithrespecttothe$l$-throw,wehave\begin{equation}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{k+l}z_{1}^{m-(\mu^{\prime}_{k}-1)-l+k}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]_{i\neq l,j\neq k}=\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-(i-\delta_{il})+j}\bigr].\end{equation}Thisiszerofor$l=2,\ldots,n$sincethe$l$-thand$(l-1)$-strowsareequal.Thuswehaveshownthattheright-handsideof\eqref{eq:S_w rel reduced}is$n\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m+\delta_{i1}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]$,andnowitisenoughtoprove\begin{equation}z_{1}*\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr]\overset{?}{=}\det\nolimits_{*}\bigl[z_{1}^{m+\delta_{i1}-\mu^{\prime}_{j}-i+j}\bigr].\end{equation}Butthisisobvioussincetwomatriceshereareoftheform\begin{equation}\begin{pmatrix}1&*\\
\textbf{0}&Z\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad\begin{pmatrix}z_{1}&*\\
\textbf{0}&Z\end{pmatrix},\end{equation}respectively,withthecommon$(n-1)\times(n-1)$matrix$Z=\bigl[z_{1}^{m-\mu^{\prime}_{i}-i+j}\bigr]_{2\leq i,j\leq n}$.Hencetheproofiscomplete.\qed\end@proof\par\par\thebibliography\lx@bibitem{Bachmann2018}H.~Bachmann,InterpolatedSchurmultiplezetavalues,\textit{J.\ Aust.\ Math.\ Soc.}\textbf{104}(2018),289--307.\lx@bibitem{Granville1997}A.~Granville,AdecompositionofRiemann^{\prime}szeta-function,Analyticnumbertheory(Kyoto,1996),95--101,LondonMath.\ Soc.\ LectureNoteSer.,247,CambridgeUniv.\ Press,Cambridge,1997.\lx@bibitem{Hoffman92}M.~E.~Hoffman,Multipleharmonicseries,\textit{Pacific J.\ Math.}\textbf{152}(2)(1992),275--290.\lx@bibitem{Hoffman97}M.~E.~Hoffman,Thealgebraofmultipleharmonicseries,\textit{J.\ Algebra}\textbf{194}(1997),477--495.\lx@bibitem{KanekoYamamoto2018}M.~KanekoandS.~Yamamoto,Anewintegral-seriesidentityofmultiplezetavaluesandregularizations.\textit{Sel.\ Math.\ New Ser.}\textbf{24}(2018),2499--2521.\lx@bibitem{Chen15}Kwang-WuChen,Applicationsofstuffleproductofmultiplezetavalues,\textit{Journal of Number Theory},\textbf{153}(2015),107--116.\lx@bibitem{NakasujiPhuksuwanYamasaki2018}M.~Nakasuji,O.~PhuksuwanandY.~Yamasaki,OnSchurmultiplezetafunctions:acombinatoricgeneralizationofmultiplezetafunctions,\textit{Adv.\ Math.}\textbf{333}(2018),570--619.\lx@bibitem{Yamamoto13}S.~Yamamoto,Explicitevaluationofcertainsumsofmultiplezeta-starvalues,\textit{Functiones et Approximatio}\textbf{49}(2013),283--289.\lx@bibitem{YamamotoIntegral}S.~Yamamoto,Multiplezeta-starvaluesandmultipleintegrals,Variousaspectsofmultiplezetavalues2016,\textit{RIMS K\^{o}ky\^{u}roku Bessatsu}\textbf{B68}(2017),3--14.\endthebibliography\par\@add@PDF@RDFa@triples\hbox{}\par\end{document}
